# BIOINFORMATICS: Module 4: R FOR BIOINFORMATICS - FASTA Format

## Introduction to FASTA Format

The FASTA format is a text-based format for representing nucleotide or peptide sequences. It is widely used in bioinformatics for storing and exchanging biological sequence data. The simplicity and widespread adoption of FASTA make it a fundamental format for many bioinformatics tasks.

---

### Learning Outcome 1: Understand the structure and purpose of the FASTA format.

*   **Purpose:**
    *   To represent biological sequences (DNA, RNA, proteins).
    *   To provide a simple, human-readable way to store and exchange sequence data.
    *   To associate a descriptive header (identifier) with each sequence.

*   **Structure:**
    *   Consists of one or more sequences.
    *   Each sequence begins with a **single-line description** (header).
        *   The header line starts with a **greater-than symbol (>)**.
        *   Following the ">", there is a sequence identifier and often a description of the sequence.
        *   There should be **no spaces** immediately after the ">".
    *   The **sequence data** follows the header line.
        *   Sequence data can be broken into multiple lines.
        *   Lines containing sequence data should **not** start with ">".
        *   Standard IUPAC codes for nucleotides and amino acids are used.
        *   Whitespace (spaces, tabs) within the sequence data is typically ignored or removed by most parsers.
        *   Lines of sequence data are usually of a fixed length (e.g., 60 or 80 characters) for readability, but this is not a strict requirement.

*   **Key Components:**
    *   **Header Line:** Identifies the sequence (e.g., gene name, accession number, species).
    *   **Sequence Data:** The actual biological sequence (nucleotides or amino acids).

*   **Example:**

```fasta
>Seq1_GeneA_Human Description of gene A from Homo sapiens

AGCTAGCTAGCTAGCTAGCTAGCTAGCTAGCTAGCTAGCTAGCTAGCTAGCTAGCTAGCT
AGCTAGCTAGCTAGCTAGCTAGCTAGCTAGCTAGCTAGCTAGCTAGCTAGCTAGCTAGCT
>Seq2_ProteinB_Mouse Sequence of protein B from Mus musculus
